# Fortress gap ledger

The gap ledger records, one claim to a row, where the revival's tree departs from Fortress as its specification and the team's papers describe it: what fails, what works that a source doubted, and what a source claimed wrongly. Each row carries its status, its kind and area, the specification's word on it, a reproducer, and who found it; the open rows are the bug reports that the work fixes. A row keeps its number for good, wherever it sits: a fixed or repeated row becomes one line in its place, and the full earlier line of every changed row is in `explorations/fortress-gap-ledger-history.md`.

Read a row with `explorations/coordinator/tools/facts-extract.sh 'ledger:N'`, and find rows with `explorations/coordinator/tools/facts-extract.sh 'ledger-find:WORDS'`; do not read this file whole. Write it only with `explorations/coordinator/tools/ledger.py` (`add FILE --section TITLE`, `note`, `close`, `duplicate`), which checks every line it writes against the template below and keeps the history; `ledger.py count` prints the counts by status, kind and area, and `ledger.py check` lints the whole file.

## The row template

A row is one line of eight cells, `| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |`, at most 1,200 characters, in the table of its topic section, in number order. A pipe inside a cell is written `\|`. (POSITIONS, "The gap ledger's form"; the proposal behind it is `explorations/coordinator/process-engineering/gap-ledger-archaeology.md`, section 4.)

- **#**: the row's number, the next free one when it is added; never reused, renumbered or given to another row. 148 is vacant.
- **claim**: at most 300 characters, whose first 150 name the defect or the capability: what fails or what holds, and where.
- **status**: exactly one word of seven, with no second status and nothing in brackets:
  - `POSITIVE-VERIFIED`: it works; it ran and its output is recorded.
  - `NEGATIVE-VERIFIED`: the specification settles it and the reproducer confirms it. A row with one half fixed stays `NEGATIVE-VERIFIED`, and its notes say which half is fixed.
  - `NEGATIVE-BOUNDED`: the failure reproduces; the mechanisms tried are named and are not exhaustive.
  - `CONTESTED`: unsettled; the notes say why.
  - `RETIRED`: a source's claim falsified here, kept so that it is not derived again.
  - `FIXED`: closed by a landed commit (`ledger.py close`): the claim's first sentence, the test as reproducer, notes `fixed HASH; full text: history, row N`.
  - `DUPLICATE`: a pointer (`ledger.py duplicate`), notes `duplicate of row M; full text: history, row N`.
- **class**: `kind (area)`; `—` only for `POSITIVE-VERIFIED` and `RETIRED`. The kind is one of `implementation gap`, `library gap vs spec`, `library bug`, `design limit`, `deliberate`, `typesetter`, `packaging`; the area one of `parser`, `walk`, `checker`, `codegen`, `runtime`, `library`, `prelude`, `specification`, `tests`, `tools`.
- **spec citation**: the `.tex` file and the section heading in quotes, as `basic/operators/juxtameaning.tex`, "Juxtaposition"; a line number only against `Specification-1.0-frozen/`; `silent` where the specification does not say.
- **reproducer**: one test (its name, or its path under the test folders; an `XXX` test while it is an expected failure) or one probe path from the repository root, at most 200 characters; `none` when nothing in the tree reproduces it, with the command and its output, or the probe's old path, in the notes.
- **found by**: the stream that found it (`climb batch 8 rung I`, `run-c3`, `apl rung 2`), at most 60 characters; no date and no role.
- **notes / workaround**: at most 700 characters: `siblings: N, M` first if there are any; the mechanism, with file:line and the commit it was true at; the workaround; for a closed row, `fixed HASH`.

A path under `explorations/` that is no longer in the tree is read from history: `git show $(git log -1 --diff-filter=D --format=%h -- PATH)^:PATH`.

## 1. Syntax: lexing, expressions and declarations

The parser and walk's reading of program text: tokens, operator words and reserved words, subscripts and superscripts after a primary, and the declaration forms with their naming and shadowing rules.

| 1 | `x[i][j]` and `x[i]^2` are grammatical, but walk invokes a subscript followed by a subscript or a superscript without its first index: one index fails with `args = ()`, more silently give the wrong element | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (walk) | `appendices/grammars/concrete-syntax.tex`, "Expressions"; `basic/operators/precedence.tex`, "Operator Precedence and Associativity"; `basic/operators/juxtameaning.tex`, "Juxtaposition" | none | astra, blinded, review | Such a primary parses as a `MathPrimary` (not `SubscriptExpr`); `Evaluator.java:1052` sends its subscript to `evalAndInvokeMethod`, whose `evalInvocationArgs` (`Evaluator.java:1251-1252`) takes `exprs.subList(1,…)`, right for a juxtaposition list and wrong for a subscript list. `m[3,7]^2` silently prints `11449`, `(m[7])^2`, not `1075369` (`explorations/gap-ledger-probes/g02.fss`). ## 2. Types: generics, static parameters, inference and coercion

Type parameters and nat (size) parameters, how walk and the compiled checker infer and bind them, their run-time descriptors on the compiled path, dimensions, and coercion.

## 5. Overloading on the compiled path: checker and dispatch

The compiled overloading checker's rules and their state, functional methods, overrides and inherited generic methods, and the code generator's dispatch among generic arms.

## 6. Integer arithmetic

The fixed-width and unbounded integer types on both paths: overflow and wrapping, division and its relatives, powers, shifts, narrowing and widening, signed and unsigned.

## 7. Numerals, floats and comparisons

What a numeral denotes on each path, the float types, floor, ceiling and rounding, rationals, and total comparisons, equality, MIN and MAX.

| 94 | the spec's constant `pi` is not in the default library: it is a `FloatLiteral` in the optional `Constants` API, not the spec's `RationalValueTimesPi` object, and the Unicode name is undefined | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | library gap vs spec (library) | `Specification/basic/expressions/literals.tex`, "Pi" | none | run-b | siblings: 95. Bare: `Variable pi is not defined.`; the Unicode spelling: `Variable ? is not defined.` (the interpreter cannot echo the character). Workaround: `import Constants.{pi}` (`Library/Constants.fss:15`): `pi = 3.141592653589793`, `pi r^2 = 12.566370614359172`. Probes `explorations/run-b/probes/worker/w06_pi_bare.fss`, `w06_pi_unicode.fss` (fail), `w06_pi.fss` and `explorations/run-b/probes/c21_constants_pi.fss` (work), in the parent of ed6dafb52. full text: history, row 94 |

## 8. Generators, reductions and ranges

Big operators and reductions, the generator protocol, the typing of the library's reduction devices and generic collections, and ranges.

| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 39 | the specification's single-declaration big operator, `opr BIG Op[\T\](g:(Reduction[\R0\],T->R0)->R0):R`, does not work: the desugarer emits a nullary operator reference | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (walk) | `Specification-1.0-frozen/basic/expressions/reductions.tex:27-34`, "Summations and Other Reduction Expressions"; `Specification-1.0-frozen/advanced/subscripting.tex:115-118`, "Big Operator Declarations" | none | ours | A big operator declared in that form dies with `got arg (): () of type ()`: `visitAccumulator` (`PreTypeCheckDesugaringVisitor.java:358-373` at 052a7c3fe) refers to the operator with no arguments. The nullary + `BigOperator` + `__bigOperatorSugar` protocol is the library's invention; the specification never describes it, and declares a big operator "as a usual operator declaration". Probe `explorations/spec-probes/p11_bigspec.fss`, in the parent of `e879b6a44`. |
| 40 | a big-operator name admits exactly one nullary declaration per program, so Σ is not extensible by addition: a user `opr SUM()` beside the library's is refused | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | design limit (walk) | `Specification-1.0-frozen/basic/overloading.tex:99-105`, "Principles of Overloading" | none | ours, astra, blinded | Two nullary declarations have the same empty value parameter list, and static parameters must agree and do not decide overloading, so walk refuses the pair: `fails because their parameter lists have the same types`. A consequence of row 39's nullary protocol; two more: `BIG CUP` from `Set` and `Map` cannot coexist in one component (row 101), and a user big operator must be written in the library's two-declaration form (row 137). Probe `explorations/spec-probes/p6_sum.fss`, in the parent of `e879b6a44`. |

## 9. Arrays, vectors and matrices

Array literals, pasting, comprehensions and factories, and the library's Vector, Matrix and rank-3 arrays: their algebra, views, subscripts and assignment.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Transpose ships only as the method `.t()`. Probes `explorations/libvector-probes/p25_spec_gaps.fss` and `p26_matrix_norm.fss`, in the parent of `e879b6a44`. |

## 10. Strings, maps and printing

Strings and their operators, maps and their big operators, and how each path renders a value as text.

| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 134 | `Map.union(f, other)` with a three-argument combining function works, including over matrix-valued maps; bare `UNION` keeps the left value | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | — | silent | none | run-b2 | The overlapping key holds the matrix sum, non-overlapping keys pass through, `\|u\| = 3`. The combiner is `(k: String, a: T, b: T) -> T` (`Library/Map.fsi:66`) — the key is passed and may be ignored. A fold of `union` over a list of maps sums many gradient environments, which is how run-b2 builds a parameter record around row 96. Probe `explorations/run-b2/probes/g1e_union.fss`, in the parent of `22ea0cd90`. |
| 135 | `BIG UNION` over a generator of maps fails unless the static arguments are given explicitly | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (walk) | `Specification/basic/expressions/reductions.tex`, "Summations and Other Reduction Expressions" | none | run-b2 | siblings: 20, 49, 424. Without them: `Failed to find any matching overload, args = (EmptyMap[\BOTTOM,BOTTOM\],NodeMap[\String,ZZ32\])`, raised inside `Library/Map.fss:198`: the reduction's empty element is built at `BOTTOM` and never unifies with the first real map. Workaround: write them, as the team does; `BIG UNION[\String,ZZ32\][ m <- seq(ss) ] m` prints `{a\|->1, b\|->2, c\|->30}` (`explorations/run-b2/probes/g1e_bigunion2.fss`); map literals need the same. The library's eleven clause-form sums write theirs (`SUM[\T\]`, `SUM[\ZZ32\]`; `d846e3644`). ## 11. Parallelism, exceptions and control flow

Implicit parallelism, spawn, atomic and transactions, and the control forms: try and catch, typecase, case, if with a generator, label and exit.

By reading, consistent with the verifier: `CodeGen.forTry` (`CodeGen.java:2019`) emits the handler inline and the JVM clears the operand stack at a handler, so the value pushed before the `try` is gone on the `catch` path, which reaches the join with one value where the normal path has two. Row 322's fix, in `forAtomicBlock` and `generateTaskCompute`, does not reach it. Also for a `try` that throws nothing; as the first operand it runs. Walk prints `ac`. Workaround: bind the `try` to a local first. Fix: save the operand stack to locals before the region and restore it at the join. full text: history, row 526 |

Outside a typecase the disambiguator refuses such a name, 'Nonesuch is undefined.'; in a typecase clause it suppresses that error (`TypeDisambiguator.java:376-380`), since a bare undeclared name there is bound to the value, of type `Any` (`:234-238`). An applied name is neither bound nor refused and reaches the trait table (`TypeAnalyzer.scala:725`). Fix: the disambiguator reports an undeclared name in a typecase arm that the binding rewrite does not take. full text: history, row 626 |

The frames per Fortress call were not measured. Workaround: `fastTrack` pins `JAVA_FLAGS` to `-Xmx4g -Xss64m` (`build.xml`, `840368f92`). `explorations/coordinator/process-engineering/build-efficiency.md` section 6.1. |

## 12. The compiled path: constructs, crashes and the switch-over

What the code generator cannot compile and where the compiled checker crashes, the compiler's own library and prelude against the interpreter's, and the one library's declarations that the checker or the specification refuses.

## 13. Syntax extension: grammars, templates and sub-languages

User grammars declared in apis, the preparser, templates and gaps, hygiene and expansion errors, compiling a grammar, and the APL sub-language built on them.

| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 179 | whitespace in a grammar production is optional at the use site: a literal space between symbols and an explicit `SPACE` both translate to the nonterminal `w` | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | — | silent | none | apl rung 1 | `nospace⦇5,5⦈` and `nospace⦇ 5 , 5 ⦈` both print `10`, which lets the book's `2 4⍴⍳8` parse against a production written with spaces. `Syntax.rats:343-345` lexes `SPACE`; `ComposingSyntaxDefTranslator.java:147` maps it and a literal space to `w`. The converse is the limit: a sub-grammar cannot require whitespace, so APL strand notation rests on the greediness of `LiteralExpr`, not on `SPACE`. Verified on the retired first library, in a worktree of `0ff5eae27~1`; probe `explorations/apl/v1-probes/p02b_mech.fss`, in the parent of 1ee3b0bc5. full text: history, row 179 |
| 180 | one production serves `f/` for **every** function glyph: a glyph-table gap followed by the reduce slash, and the same with `⌿` | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | — | silent | none | apl rung 1 | `redop⦇ +/ 3 ⦈`, `⍳/`, `*/` and `redfi⦇ +⌿ 3 ⦈` all reduce to one call with the glyph's name, so APL's reduce is one rule, not one per glyph, though the `?`-bound gap that would make the slash optional is unimplemented (row 274). Measured on the retired first library; probe `explorations/apl/v1-probes/p02b_mech.fss`, gone. The current base writes one rule per glyph (`explorations/apl/base/AplSyntax.fsi:419-422`). full text: history, row 180 |
| 181 | the preparser reads a backtick escape in a grammar production as an opening quote wanting a closing `'` and rejects the api; the macro language's own special characters, APL's `+` and `*` among them, need that escape | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | implementation gap (parser) | silent | none | apl rung 1 | siblings: 211. `Unmatched delimiter "api".` and ``Unmatched delimiters "`" and "end".`` `PreCompilation.rats:115` puts the backtick in `Opening`; `PreParserState.java:256` pairs it only with `'` or `’`. Workaround: an APL glyph above the escape: the preparser cannot tokenise it, its token loop (`PreCompilation.rats:56`, no end-of-file anchor) stops, and `report()` never runs; `explorations/apl/base/AplSyntax.fsi` keeps `⌿ ⍬ ⍎` above its escaped `+`. Inside `⦇ ⦈` it also breaks their pairing. Retired first library, worktree of `0ff5eae27~1`; probes `explorations/apl/v1-probes/p08a_g.fsi` (fails), `p08b_g.fsi` (works), `p08d_g.fsi`, in the parent of 1ee3b0bc5. full text: history, row 181 |
| 182 | an `Id` gap cannot be spliced into an **expression** position, parenthesised or bare | DUPLICATE | implementation gap (parser) | `Specification/advanced/domain-specific-languages.tex`, "Support for Domain-Specific Languages" | none | apl rung 1 | duplicate of row 202; full text: history, row 182 |
| 183 | an **undelimited** host `Expr` gap swallows any following terminal the host knows as an operator (`≡`, `⋄`, `∘`), because a gap is greedy and Rats never re-enters a nonterminal for a shorter match | NEGATIVE-VERIFIED | design limit (parser) | silent | none | apl rung 1 | siblings: 235. `esc2⦇ v ≡ m ⦈` makes the expander unparsable (`Variable esc2 is not defined`); `apl⦇ ⍎v ≡ 1 4⍴⍎v ⦈` parsed as `aplDy("rho", v EQV (1 4), v)`. Row 284's case: in `y⦇ a:Expr ⋄ b:Expr ⦈` the first gap takes `a ⋄ b` as the host's DIAMOND (`Literal.rats:428`), `∘` (RING, `:403-405`) likewise, leaving a call of an undefined `y` (`Variable y4 is not defined.`). Workarounds: bracket the gap (`⍎ ( SPACE e:Expr SPACE )`, written `⍎(v)`), or follow it with a sub-language nonterminal or a terminal in no host operator table (`⍎v ⍴ ⍎m` parses; `⍤` separates). Probes `explorations/apl/v1-probes/p03b_escape.fss`, `explorations/apl/probes-4b/y01_named.fss`, gone. full text: history, row 183 |
| 186 | a template cannot expand to an **assignment** to a gap: no gap on the left of `:=` parses, whether a parenthesised or bare `Expr` gap or an `Id` gap | NEGATIVE-BOUNDED | implementation gap (parser) | silent | none | apl rung 1 | siblings: 196. `MacroError: Could not parse 'do ( <!@#$%^&*<Expr t >*&^%$#@!> ) := …'` with `Syntax Error` at the `:=`; the `Id`-gap spelling gives `Parameter 'text' to the IdOrOp constructor was null`. So APL's `v ← …` can neither bind nor assign a Fortress variable through a gap there. Workaround, verified: expand to a method call, `asgnc⦇ ⍎(c) ← 41 ⦈` → `c.put(41)` on a user object with a `var` field prints `41`; a literal name on the left also assigns (row 205). ## 15. Performance and program shape

Measured costs of walk and the compiled path, and of the shapes a program can take on them.

| # | claim | status | class | spec citation | reproducer | found by | notes / workaround |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 139 | the functional-backprop shape (every node carries a map from its cotangent to the parameter cotangents, sharing summed at fan-out) is exponential in the number of fan-outs along a chain; a tape is linear | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | — | silent | none | run-b | Not a Fortress defect: it is why run-b's engine is a tape, and the two shapes cannot be told apart at depth 1. At residual depths 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10: functional 79 / 70 / 197 / 400 / 1617 / 6587 ms per forward+backward, tape 105 / 41 / 62 / 84 / 72 / 77 ms (run-b measured 45/45/135/481/1872/6950 against 53/32/46/75/68/198). Probes `explorations/run-b/probes/s03_functional_depth.fss`, `s03_tape_depth.fss`, in the parent of `ed6dafb52`; full text: history, row 139 |
| 154 | the library matrix product costs tens of microseconds per multiply-add under walk, and hand-written products are no faster: at 16K multiply-adds (64x16 . 16x16), 217 ms library, 215 ms juxtaposition, 383 ms a `fill` of `SUM`s, 325 ms a sequential loop | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | design limit (walk) | silent | none | run-c | At 65K, (64x64 . 64x16) is 884 ms library against 1259 ms as a loop, (64x16 . (64x16)^T) 890 against 1098 ms; second of two repetitions, the first up to 2x slower; about 13 us per multiply-add here, Run C's 24 us a uniform host factor. The library product is the fastest spelling, a loop 1.4-1.5x slower, and cost tracks the multiply-add count, not the shape: the library's recursive partition costs per leaf what a `for` costs per iteration. A batch-1 step is about 190K multiply-adds plus the row-wise maps; the check's finite differences, two steps per index, take 855 s at one thread. Probe `explorations/run-c/probes/p05_mmtime.fss`, in the parent of `4ebf80aec`; full text: history, row 154 |

Form: `+.×⍤2` as one `opr juxtaposition` over rank 3, `⍉⍤2` a view, `MK+⍤2` one `opr +`, `sm⍤1` the row lift over planes; four documents, three forward-backward repetitions. | 245 | every APL function value can be a zero-parameter lambda over one mutable frame stack, the only shape that survives nesting: `aplCall(f, ⍺, ⍵)` pushes, calls `f()` and pops, so no name is ever bound | POSITIVE-VERIFIED | — | silent | `explorations/apl/rung-4/u04_frame.fss` | apl rung 4 | So u01's "already declared" cannot arise; `⍺ ⍵ ∇` are frame reads, the default `⍺ ←` a write to the current frame, and APL's rule that `⍺ ⍵` are never inherited comes free.
